### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)
4. We need to handle the 24-hour cycle - times wrap from 23:59 to 00:00

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand palindrome validation
A time "hh:mm" is a palindrome if when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we get the same string.
For "05:50", reversed is "05:50" → palindrome.
For "05:39", reversed is "93:50" → not palindrome.

### Step 2: Handle the time cycle
We need to check times in chronological order, starting from the current time, and wrap around to 00:00 if needed.

### Step 3: Algorithm approach
1. Parse the input time into hours and minutes
2. Starting from current time, check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome
3. Since we want minimum minutes, we just keep incrementing until we find the first palindrome
4. If we go past 23:59, we wrap to 00:00

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Convert time to total minutes since 00:00 (0 to 1439)
- Check each time from current time (inclusive) to 1439 (23:59), then wrap to 0
- For each time, format as "hh:mm" and check if it's a palindrome
- Return the difference in minutes

### Step 5: Edge cases
- Current time is already a palindrome → 0 minutes
- Time wraps around (e.g., 23:59 → 00:00)

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads the same forwards and backwards
    time_without_colon = time_str.replace(":", "")
    return time_without_colon == time_without_colon[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hh = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    mm = total_minutes % 60
    return hh, mm

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(":"))

# Convert current time to minutes since 00:00
current_minutes = time_to_minutes(hh, mm)

# Check each time from current time onwards
# We need to check up to 24 hours (1440 minutes), but since we're in 24-hour format,
# we only need to check 1440 minutes maximum.

total_minutes = current_minutes
for _ in range(1440):  # 24 hours = 1440 minutes
    current_hh, current_mm = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
    
    # Format time as "hh:mm" with leading zeros
    time_str = f"{current_hh:02d}:{current_mm:02d}"
    
    # Check if it's a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        # Calculate minutes to sleep
        minutes_to_sleep = total_minutes - current_minutes
        if minutes_to_sleep < 0:
            minutes_to_sleep += 1440
        print(minutes_to_sleep)
        break
    
    total_minutes = (total_minutes + 1) % 1440
```

Wait, I see a simpler approach. Since we're looking for the next palindrome time, and we're guaranteed to find one within 24 hours, we can just increment from the current time.

Let me simplify and correct:

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads the same forwards and backwards
    time_without_colon = time_str.replace(":", "")
    return time_without_colon == time_without_colon[::-1]

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(":"))

# Convert current time to minutes since 00:00
current_minutes = hh * 60 + mm

# Check each time from current time onwards
# We'll check up to 24 hours (1440 minutes)
total_minutes = current_minutes
for _ in range(1440):
    current_hh = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    current_mm = total_minutes % 60
    
    # Format time as "hh:mm" with leading zeros
    time_str = f"{current_hh:02d}:{current_mm:02d}"
    
    # Check if it's a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        # The time has been found
        minutes_to_sleep = total_minutes - current_minutes
        if minutes_to_sleep < 0:
            minutes_to_sleep += 1440
        print(minutes_to_sleep)
        break
    
    total_minutes = (total_minutes + 1) % 1440
```

current_minutes = hh * 60 + mm

# Check each time from current time onwards
# We'll check up to 24 hours (1440 minutes)
total_minutes = current_minutes
for _ in range(1440):
    current_hh = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    current_mm = total_minutes % 60
    
    # Format time as "hh:mm" with leading zeros
    time_str = f"{current_hh:02d}:{current_mm:02d}"
    
    # Check if it's a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        # The time has been found
        minutes_to_sleep = total_minutes - current_minutes
        if minutes_to_sleep < 0:
            minutes_to_sleep += 1440
        print(minutes_to_sleep)
        break
    
    total_minutes = (total_minutes + 1) % 1440

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards when considered as a string
4. For example, "05:50" is a palindrome because "05:50" reversed is "05:50"

## Key Insights

1. A time is palindromic if when we reverse the string, we get the same string
2. The time format is hh:mm, so when reversed, it becomes mm:hh
3. For hh:mm to be a palindrome, reversing "hh:mm" should give us "hh:mm"
4. This means: reversing "hh:mm" = "mm:hh" should equal "hh:mm"
5. So we need hh:mm = mm:hh as strings

## Step-by-step Solution

1. First, check if the current time is already a palindrome
2. If yes, return 0
3. If not, we need to find the next palindromic time
4. Since we're in a 24-hour cycle, we need to consider if the next palindrome might wrap around to the next day (e.g., 23:59 -> 00:00)

## Algorithm

1. Convert current time to minutes since midnight
2. Check if current time is palindromic
3. If yes, return 0
4. Otherwise, iterate through possible times starting from current time + 1 minute
5. For each time, check if it's palindromic
6. Return the first palindromic time found

## Palindrome Check Function

For time "hh:mm", it's palindromic if when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we get the same string.

For example:
- "05:39" reversed = "93:50" → not equal
- "05:50" reversed = "05:50" → equal (it's a palindrome)

Wait, let me reconsider the palindrome definition. The problem says "05:50" is a palindrome because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.

So "05:50" reversed character by character should equal "05:50".

Actually, let me think about this more carefully:
- "05:50" reversed character by character = "05:50" → yes, it's palindromic
- "05:39" reversed character by character = "93:50" → not equal

So we need to check if the reversed string equals the original string.

## Implementation Steps

1. Parse the input to get hours and minutes
2. Convert to total minutes since midnight
3. Check if current time is palindromic
4. If not, increment time and check each subsequent minute until we find a palindromic time

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and reverse the string
    time_no_colon = time_str.replace(':', '')
    reversed_time = time_no_colon[::-1]
    return time_no_colon == reversed_time

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = total_minutes // 60
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours % 24, minutes

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindromic
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Convert current time to minutes since midnight
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(hh, mm)
    
    # Find the next palindromic time
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        if total_minutes >= 24 * 60:  # One full day
            total_minutes = 0
        
        hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return total_minutes - current_minutes

print(solve())
```

- Next check: "05:40" → "04:50" → not equal
- Continue until "05:50" → "05:50" reversed = "05:50" → equal → palindrome

Example 3: "23:59" → not palindrome (23:59 → 95:32)

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards when interpreted as a string
4. For example, 05:50 is a palindrome because "05:50" reversed is "05:50"

## Key Insights

1. A time "hh:mm" is a palindrome if the string "hh:mm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. When we reverse "hh:mm", we need to check if it matches the original
3. We need to find the next palindromic time (including possibly wrapping from 23:59 to 00:00)
4. The time is 24-hour format, so we need to handle the wrap-around

## Algorithm Steps

1. Parse the input time to get hours and minutes
2. Create a function to check if a time is palindromic
3. Start from the current time and increment minutes until we find a palindromic time
4. Handle the wrap-around from 23:59 to 00:00

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Parse input**: Extract hours and minutes from hh:mm format
2. **Check palindrome**: A time "hh:mm" is palindromic if when we reverse the entire string, it matches the original
3. **Find next palindrome**: Start from current time and keep adding minutes until we find a palindromic time
4. **Handle wrap-around**: When we go past 23:59, we wrap to 00:00

Let me trace through the examples:

- 05:39 → 05:50 is palindromic (05:50 reversed is 05:50) → 11 minutes
- 13:31 → already palindromic → 0 minutes  
- 23:59 → next palindrome is 00:00 (after 1 minute) → 1 minute

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads the same forwards and backwards
    time_without_colon = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return time_without_colon == time_without_colon[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

def solve(input_time):
    # Parse input
    h, m = map(int, input_time.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindromic
    if is_palindrome(input_time):
        return 0
    
    # Start from current time and find next palindromic time
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)
    
    # Check each minute in sequence until we find a palindrome
    # We need to go through the day (24 hours = 1440 minutes)
    max_minutes = 1440
    for minutes_to_add in range(1, max_minutes + 1):
        total_minutes = current_minutes + minutes_to_add
        
        # Convert total minutes to hours and minutes
        hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
        
        # Format time as string hh:mm
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        
        # Check if this time is palindromic
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return minutes_to_add
    
    # This should never happen in a 24-hour cycle
    return 0

# Read input and solve
input_time = input().strip()
result = solve(input_time)
print(result)
```

- Current time: 05:39
- Is "05:39" palindromic? "0539" reversed is "9350" ≠ "0539" → not palindromic
- Try adding 1 minute: 05:40 → "0540" reversed is "0450" ≠ "0540"
- ...
- Try adding 11 minutes: 05:50 → "0550" reversed is "0550" = "0550" → palindromic!
- Result: 11 ✓

For "13:31":
- "13:31" → "1331" reversed is "1331" → palindromic → return 0 ✓

For "23:59":
- 23:59 → "2359" reversed is "9532" ≠ "2359"
- Add 1 minute: 00:00 → "0000" reversed is "0000" → palindromic → return 1 ✓

The solution looks correct!

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backward is 05:50)
4. We need to check times in chronological order until we find a palindrome

## Key Insights

1. A time hh:mm is a palindrome if when we reverse the string "hh:mm", it reads the same
2. For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome, the reversed string should be the same
3. The reversed string of "hh:mm" would be "mm:hh" but in reverse order
4. Actually, the palindrome condition means: the string "hh:mm" should read the same forwards and backwards
5. So we need "hh:mm" to equal its reverse

Let me think about the reversal:
- Original: "hh:mm" → characters: h,h,:,m,m
- Reverse: m,m,:,h,h
- For palindrome: "hh:mm" should equal "mm:hh" when reversed

Wait, let me reconsider the example: 05:50 backward is 05:50
- 05:50 → reverse is 05:50 (same)
- The string "05:50" reversed is "05:50" (since 0,5,:,5,0 reversed is 0,5,:,5,0)

Actually, let me check: "05:50" reversed character by character:
- Original: '0','5',':','5','0'
- Reversed: '0','5',':','5','0' → same!

So the condition is that the string "hh:mm" should be equal to its reverse.

For a time to be a palindrome:
- Let's say we have hh:mm
- The string is s = str(hh) + ":" + str(mm)
- The reverse is reverse(s)
- We need s == reverse(s)

## Algorithm

1. Parse the input time to get current hour and minute
2. Starting from the current time, check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome time
3. For each time, convert to string "hh:mm" and check if it's a palindrome
4. Return the number of minutes until we find the first palindrome

## Implementation Details

1. We need to handle time wrapping (e.g., from 23:59 to 00:00)
2. We can iterate through time in minutes, converting back to hh:mm format
3. For each time, check if the string is a palindrome

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove spaces and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    s = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return s == s[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    # Convert total minutes to hh:mm format
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

def solve(current_time):
    # Parse current time
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Convert current time to total minutes since start of day
    current_minutes = h * 60 + m
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"):
        return 0
    
    # Check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        if total_minutes >= 24 * 60:  # Wrap around to next day
            total_minutes = 0  # But we don't need to go to next day, just continue from 00:00
        
        # Convert total minutes back to hh:mm
        h_next = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
        m_next = total_minutes % 60
        
        # Check if this time is a palindrome
        time_str = f"{h_next:02d}:{m_next:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return total_minutes - current_minutes
    
    # This should never be reached due to finite search space
    return 0

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

- 23:59 → 00:00 (next day) = 1 minute, which is correct

```python
        return 0

    # We need to handle time wrapping (going from 23:59 to 00:00)
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        if total_minutes >= 24 * 60:  # We've gone to next day
            # But we just need to go to 00:00 from 23:59
            # Actually, we should check if 00:00 is palindrome

            # So total_minutes should be 1 when current is 23:59
```

```python
    return s == s[::-1]

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))

# Current time in minutes since start of day
current_minutes = h * 60 + m

# Check if current time is already a palindrome
time_str = f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"
if is_palindrome(time_str):
    print(0)
else:
    # Find next palindrome time
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        # Convert to hh:mm
        h_next = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
        m_next = total_minutes % 60
        time_str = f"{h_next:02d}:{m_next:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            print(total_minutes - current_minutes)
            break
```

What is the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome?

Remember that a palindrome is a string that reads the same forwards and backwards. For instance, 05:39 is not a palindrome, because 05:39 backwards is 93:50. On the other hand, 05:50 is a palindrome, because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.


-----Input-----

The first and only line of input contains a single string in the format hh:mm (00 ≤  hh  ≤ 23, 00 ≤  mm  ≤ 59).


-----Output-----

-----Examples-----
Input
05:39

Output
11

Input
13:31

Output
0

Input
23:59

Output
1



-----Note-----

In the first test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 11. She can wake up at 05:50, when the time is a palindrome.

In the second test case, Karen can wake up immediately, as the current time, 13:31, is already a palindrome.

In the third test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 1 minute. She can wake up at 00:00, when the time is a palindrome.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards when considering the string format
4. For example, 05:50 is a palindrome because "05:50" reversed is "05:50"
5. We need to consider the next palindromic time (could be the same time, or later in the day)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand palindrome checking
For a time "hh:mm", when reversed, we get a string that should match the original.
- "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → palindrome ✓
- "05:39" reversed is "93:50" → not palindrome ✗

### Step 2: Approach
1. Convert the current time to minutes since midnight
2. Check if current time is already palindromic - if yes, return 0
3. Otherwise, check future times (from next minute onwards) until we find a palindromic time
4. Return the difference in minutes

### Step 3: Implementation details
- Parse input to get hours and minutes
- Convert current time to total minutes since midnight
- Create a function to check if a time is palindromic
- Start from current time and iterate forward until we find a palindrome
- Since we're working in 24-hour format, we don't need to wrap around (the next palindrome will be within 24 hours)

### Step 4: Palindrome checking function
For time "hh:mm", when we reverse the string we get a new string. This should be the same as original for it to be a palindrome.

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string is palindrome
    time_str_no_colon = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return time_str_no_colon == time_str_no_colon[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(minutes):
    hh = minutes // 60
    mm = minutes % 60
    return hh % 24, mm

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))

# Convert to minutes since midnight
current_minutes = time_to_minutes(hh, mm)

# Check if current time is already palindromic
if is_palindrome(time_str):
    print(0)
else:
    # Start from next minute and check each time until we find palindrome
    next_minutes = current_minutes + 1
    total_minutes = 24 * 60  # Maximum minutes in a day
    
    while next_minutes < total_minutes:
        hh, mm = minutes_to_time(next_minutes)
        time_formatted = f"{hh:02d}:{mm:02d}"
        if is_palindrome(time_formatted):
            print(next_minutes - current_minutes)
            break
        next_minutes += 1
```

- Next time: 05:40 → "0540" reversed is "0450" ≠ "0540" → not palindrome
- Continue until 05:50 → "0550" reversed is "0550" = "0550" → palindrome
- Difference: 11 minutes ✓

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (like 05:50 → 05:50)

## Key Observations

1. A palindrome time in format hh:mm must satisfy: hh:mm reads the same as mm:hh when reversed
2. For hh:mm to be a palindrome, when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we should get the same string
3. The reversed string of "hh:mm" would be "mm:hh" (but with the digits reversed in order)

Wait, let me reconsider the palindrome condition. Looking at the examples:
- 05:39 is not a palindrome because reversed is 93:50
- 05:50 is a palindrome because reversed is 05:50

So for a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome, when we reverse the entire string "hh:mm", we should get the same string.

The string "hh:mm" has format like "05:39". When reversed, it becomes "93:50" (as shown in the problem).

For "05:50" to be a palindrome: 
- Original: "05:50"
- Reversed: "05:50" → same! 

So the palindrome condition is that the string reads the same forwards and backwards.

## Algorithm Approach

1. Parse the input time to get current hour and minute
2. Start from the current time and increment minute by minute until we find a palindrome time
3. Check if a time is palindrome by:
   - Convert hh:mm to string "hh:mm"
   - Reverse the string
   - Check if reversed string equals original string

However, since we can wrap around from 23:59 to 00:00, we need to handle the 24-hour cycle.

## Implementation Strategy

Since the answer can't be very large (at most 24 hours = 1440 minutes), we can simply iterate from the current time forward until we find a palindrome.

But we need to handle the time wrapping. We can work with minutes of day:

1. Convert current time to total minutes from midnight
2. Check times in sequence, taking modulo 1440 to handle wrapping
3. For each time, check if it's a palindrome

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Parse input to get hours and minutes
2. Convert current time to total minutes from midnight
3. Start checking from current time onwards, incrementing by 1 minute each time
4. For each time, convert back to hh:mm format and check if it's a palindrome
5. Return the number of minutes needed

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # time_str is in format "hh:mm"
    # Check if it reads the same forwards and backwards
    s = time_str.replace(":", "")
    return s == s[::-1]

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Convert current time to total minutes from midnight
    current_minutes = hours * 60 + minutes
    
    # Total minutes in a day
    total_minutes = 24 * 60
    
    # Check each minute from current time onwards
    # We need to handle wrapping around midnight
    for minutes_to_sleep in range(total_minutes):
        # Calculate target time in minutes from midnight
        target_minutes = (current_minutes + minutes_to_sleep) % total_minutes
        
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        target_hour = (target_minutes // 60) % 24
        target_minute = target_minutes % 60
        
        # Format as hh:mm with leading zeros
        target_time_str = f"{target_hour:02d}:{target_minute:02d}"
        
        # Check if it's a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(target_time_str):
            return minutes_to_sleep
    
    return 0  # This should never happen

print(solve())
```

Wait, let me verify this with the examples:

Example 1: 05:39
- Current time: 5*60 + 39 = 339 minutes
- We check 339, 340, 341, ... until we find a palindrome
- At 05:50: hours=5, minutes=50, time_str="05:50"
- "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → palindrome!
- Minutes to sleep = (300 + 11) - 339 = 311 - 339 = -28? 

I made an error in calculation. Let me recalculate:

05:39 to 05:50 is 11 minutes. So minutes_to_sleep should be 11.

In my loop:
- target_minutes = (339 + minutes_to_sleep) % 1440
- At minutes_to_sleep = 11: target_minutes = 350
- target_hour = 350 // 60 = 5, target_minute = 350 % 60 = 50
- target_time_str = "05:50"
- "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → palindrome!

So it works. The calculation is correct.

Example 2: 13:31
- Current time: 13*60 + 31 = 811 minutes
- Check if 13:31 is palindrome: "13:31" reversed is "13:31" → actually wait, "13:31" reversed is "13:31" backwards = "13:31" → actually check: 
- "13:31" reversed = "13:31" → same? No, "13:31" backwards is "13:31" → actually letters: "1","3",":","3","1" → reversed is "1","3","3","1" → which is "13:31" backwards is "1331" → which is not "13:31" in the same format.

Wait, I think I'm confused. The palindrome condition is on the string "hh:mm".

"13:31" -> reverse the string: "13:31" backwards is "13:31" → "13:31" reversed is "13:31" → let me write it: 
Original: "13:31" 
Position: 1,3,:,3,1
Reversed: 1,3,3,1,3,1 → no!

"13:31" reversed character by character: 
- Original: "1", "3", ":", "3", "1"
- Reversed: "1", "3", ":", "3", "1" → "13:31" → same!

Actually: 
"13:31" reversed is "13:31" → the first character becomes last, etc.
So "1" -> last, "3" -> second last, ":" -> third last, "3" -> fourth last, "1" -> fifth last
So reversed is "1", "3", ":", "3", "1" → "13:31" → same!

"13:31" is a palindrome!

So the algorithm is correct.

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Check if string reads the same forwards and backwards
    s = time_str
    return s == s[::-1]

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
hours, minutes = map(int, time_str.split(':'))

# Convert current time to total minutes from midnight
current_minutes = hours * 60 + minutes

# Total minutes in a day
total_minutes = 24 * 60

# Check each minute from current time onwards
for minutes_to_sleep in range(total_minutes):
    # Calculate target time in minutes from midnight
    target_minutes = (current_minutes + minutes_to_sleep) % total_minutes
    
    # Convert back to hours and minutes
    target_hour = (target_minutes // 60) % 24
    target_minute = target_minutes % 60
    
    # Format as hh:mm with leading zeros
    target_time_str = f"{target_hour:02d}:{target_minute:02d}"
    
    # Check if it's a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(target_time_str):
        print(minutes_to_sleep)
        break
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes until the next palindromic time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 when reversed)

## Key Insights

1. A time "hh:mm" is a palindrome if the string "hhmm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome: the first digit of hh should equal the last digit of mm, and the second digit of hh should equal the first digit of mm
3. We need to check times starting from the current time and going forward until we find a palindrome
4. We need to handle the case where going forward crosses midnight (e.g., 23:59 → 00:00)

## Step-by-Step Approach

1. Parse the input time into hours and minutes
2. Create a function to check if a time is a palindrome
3. Start from the current time and keep adding minutes until we find a palindromic time
4. Handle the case where we go past 23:59 and wrap to 00:00

## Implementation Details

For a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome:
- The string "hhmm" should be equal to its reverse
- So: first digit of hh = last digit of mm
- Second digit of hh = first digit of mm

## Edge Cases

- Current time might already be palindromic (return 0)
- After midnight crossing (e.g., 23:59 → 00:00)

```python
def is_palindrome_time(hh, mm):
    # Format as string: hhmm
    time_str = f"{hh:02d}{mm:02d}"
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def solve(current_time):
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    if is_palindrome_time(h, m):
        return 0
    
    # Start from current time and go forward minute by minute
    minutes = 0
    
    # We'll simulate time increasing until we find a palindrome
    # We need to check times from current time onwards
    current_h, current_m = h, m
    
    while True:
        minutes += 1
        current_m += 1
        
        # Handle minute overflow
        if current_m >= 60:
            current_m = 0
            current_h += 1
            
            # Handle hour overflow (cross midnight)
            if current_h >= 24:
                current_h = 0
    
        # Check if current time is palindrome
        if is_palindrome_time(current_h, current_m):
            return minutes

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

Wait, I see a potential issue with my approach. Let me reconsider.

The above approach might miss the case where we need to go backwards through midnight. However, since we want the minimum time until the next palindromic time, and palindromic times are relatively rare, we should just go forward in time.

However, the third example shows 23:59 → 00:00 in 1 minute, which suggests we need to handle the midnight wrapping correctly.

Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1: 05:39 → 05:50
- 05:39: is "0539" → reverse is "9350" → not palindrome
- 05:40: "0540" → reverse "0450" → not palindrome
- ...
- 05:50: "0550" → reverse "0550" → palindrome ✓
- So we need 11 minutes

Example 2: 13:31 → 13:31
- "1331" → reverse "1331" → palindrome ✓
- So answer is 0

Example 3: 23:59 → 00:00
- "2359" → reverse "9532" → not palindrome
- Next: 00:00 → "0000" → reverse "0000" → palindrome ✓
- So we need 1 minute

My algorithm looks correct, but let me fix the implementation to avoid infinite loops and ensure correctness.

```python
def is_palindrome_time(hh, mm):
    # Format as string: hhmm
    time_str = f"{hh:02d}{mm:02d}"
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))

# Check if current time is already palindrome
if is_palindrome_time(h, m):
    print(0)
else:
    minutes = 0
    current_h, current_m = h, m
    
    # We need to find the next palindromic time
    while True:
        minutes += 1
        current_m += 1
        
        # Handle minute overflow
        if current_m >= 60:
            current_m = 0
            current_h += 1
            
            # Handle hour overflow (cross midnight)
            if current_h >= 24:
                current_h = 0
        
        # Check if current time is palindrome
        if is_palindrome_time(current_h, current_m):
            print(minutes)
            break
```

def is_palindrome_time(hh, mm):
    # Format as string: hhmm
    time_str = f"{hh:02d}{mm:02d}"
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))

# Check if current time is already palindrome
if is_palindrome_time(h, m):
    print(0)
else:
    minutes = 0
    current_h, current_m = h, m
    
    # We need to find the next palindromic time
    while True:
        minutes += 1
        current_m += 1
        
        # Handle minute overflow
        if current_m >= 60:
            current_m = 0
            current_h += 1
            
            # Handle hour overflow (cross midnight)
            if current_h >= 24:
                current_h = 0
        
        # Check if current time is palindrome
        if is_palindrome_time(current_h, current_m):
            print(minutes)
            break

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Karen is getting ready for a new school day!

 [Image] 

It is currently hh:mm, given in a 24-hour format. As you know, Karen loves palindromes, and she believes that it is good luck to wake up when the time is a palindrome.

What is the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome?

Remember that a palindrome is a string that reads the same forwards and backwards. For instance, 05:39 is not a palindrome, because 05:39 backwards is 93:50. On the other hand, 05:50 is a palindrome, because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.


-----Input-----

The first and only line of input contains a single string in the format hh:mm (00 ≤  hh  ≤ 23, 00 ≤  mm  ≤ 59).


-----Output-----

Output a single integer on a line by itself, the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome.


-----Examples-----
Input
05:39

Output
11

Input
13:31

Output
0

Input
23:59

Output
1



-----Note-----

In the first test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 11. She can wake up at 05:50, when the time is a palindrome.

In the second test case, Karen can wake up immediately, as the current time, 13:31, is already a palindrome.

In the third test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 1 minute. She can wake up at 00:00, when the time is a palindrome.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout


Now solve the problem and return the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (when we consider the string representation)

## Key Insights

1. A time "hh:mm" is a palindrome if the string "hhmm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For example, "05:50" becomes "0550" which is a palindrome since "0550" reversed is "0550"
3. We need to check times starting from the current time and moving forward in minutes until we find a palindrome
4. Since we're dealing with 24-hour format, we might need to wrap around to the next day (like from 23:59 to 00:00)

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. Parse the input time into hours and minutes
2. Convert the current time to total minutes since 00:00
3. Start checking times from the current time onwards (in 1-minute increments)
4. For each time, convert it back to hh:mm format and check if it's a palindrome
5. Return the number of minutes from current time to first palindrome

## Implementation Details

- A time is a palindrome if the string "hhmm" reads the same forwards and backwards
- We need to handle the case where we might wrap around to the next day
- We can check each minute increment until we find a palindrome

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # time_str is in format "hhmm"
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

def solve(current_time):
    # Parse input
    h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Convert current time to total minutes since 00:00
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)
    
    # Check each minute from current time onwards
    # We need to check until we find a palindrome
    # Since we're looking for minimum time, we go forward in minutes
    
    # We might need to wrap to next day, so we check a reasonable number of minutes
    # Worst case is 119 minutes (from 23:59 to 00:00, then to 00:00 is 1 minute, 
    # but from 23:59 to next palindrome at 00:00 is 1 minute)
    
    # We can safely check up to 120 minutes since the maximum gap between palindromes
    # in 24-hour format is at most 119 minutes (from 23:59 to 00:00)
    
    for minutes_to_add in range(0, 120):
        total_minutes = current_minutes + minutes_to_add
        hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
        
        # Format hours and minutes with leading zeros
        hh = hours
        mm = minutes
        
        # Create time string in format "hhmm"
        time_str = f"{hh:02d}{mm:02d}"
        
        # Check if it's a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            return minutes_to_add
    
    # This should never happen in valid input
    return 0

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve(current_time))
```

Wait, let me verify this with the examples:

Example 1: 05:39
- Current time: 05:39 → 5*60 + 39 = 339 minutes
- Try 05:39 → "0539" → reverse = "9350" → not palindrome
- Try 05:40 → "0540" → reverse = "0450" → not palindrome
- ...
- Try 05:50 → "0550" → reverse = "0550" → palindrome!
- Minutes to add: 50 - 39 = 11 → correct!

Example 2: 13:31
- Current time: 13:31 → 13*60 + 31 = 811 minutes
- Time "1331" → reverse = "1331" → palindrome!
- Minutes to add: 0 → correct!

Example 3: 23:59
- Current time: 23:59 → 23*60 + 59 = 1439 minutes
- Try 23:59 → "2359" → reverse = "9532" → not palindrome
- Next time: 00:00 → "0000" → reverse = "0000" → palindrome!
- Minutes to add: (00:00 is 0 minutes) → (0 - 1439) mod 1440 = 1 → correct!

The solution looks correct.

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # time_str is in format "hhmm"
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
h, m = map(int, current_time.split(':'))

# Convert current time to total minutes since 00:00
current_minutes = time_to_minutes(h, m)

# Check each minute from current time onwards
# The maximum gap between palindromes is less than 120 minutes
for minutes_to_add in range(0, 120):
    total_minutes = current_minutes + minutes_to_add
    hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
    
    # Format hours and minutes with leading zeros
    hh = hours
    mm = minutes
    
    # Create time string in format "hhmm"
    time_str = f"{hh:02d}{mm:02d}"
    
    # Check if it's a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        print(minutes_to_add)
        break
```
